# Pancake test ledger

Every test run on this fork, one row per test (spec run, measurement, parity/ctest run, offline replay, quality gate, profile), from 2026-11-07 to 2026-11-15. This file is the only record of test results; MOD_PLAN.md cites rows by id (`T-123`). Add a row for every new run (next free id) in the same commit as the run.

How to read a row:
- **id / date**: ids are stable; new tests get the next free id at the end of their area table. A date starting with `~` is inferred.
- **tier/reps**: screen (2-4 repeats) or confirm (6-12 repeats), paired A B B A unless noted; run 1 discarded.
- **arms -> result**: decode tok/s change vs the base arm per case (`short`, `edit`, `longdec10k`, `longdec32k`, `long1k`/`long3k` prompt cases), with the 95% interval when the harness printed one. "no clean runs" = every run failed the noise filter.
- **exact/text**: whether the greedy text matched the base. Text identity is not an exactness proof (stock-vs-stock noise is 1.2-1.3% of tokens); token forcing (`tools/pancake/quality_gate.py`) is the quality check.
- **verdict**: ADOPTED (in a preset or the default path), DROPPED, PROMOTED-CONFIRM-PENDING (passed a screen, confirm or gate missing), OPEN-NO-DECISION (measured, nobody decided), PASS / FAIL (parity, ctest, gates), ABORTED (run broke), INFO (measurement with no decision attached).
- **archive**: raw output kept in the repo: `bench/specs/*.json` (the spec), `bench/results/<date>-*/` and `pancake-docs/research/bench/results-2026-11-1x/` (jsonl, stdout, summaries, profiles). "no archive" = no raw output was kept; the number in the row is the record.

Hardware: RX 7900 XT 20 GB (gfx1100, ROCm 10.0) + Ryzen 7 5700G unless the hw column says otherwise; rows marked 3060 / sm_86 were run on the RTX 3060 before it was removed on 2026-11-08.

UNCAPPED (T-618): kmiss 2/8 16.89 ms / 86.51% / 81.6 promotions per window (re-run after the fix: identical). So the 1.6-1.7 ms that kmiss 2/4 and 2/8 gained over the count tier in T-618..T-621 was the swap cap, not the rule: tier is capped at 32 swaps a window like the preset, kmiss was not (engine: generate.cpp 14843-14844 caps both). Defect found and fixed in tools/pancake/tier_laws.py: a capped KMiss run tripped the residency assertion because the policy's own LRU list assumed every proposal was applied (now: candidates scored like kmiss_tier.hpp, 1000 x misses + usage, the best cap kept, only kept swaps change the lists; test_kmiss_with_cap_keeps_its_lists_consistent_with_the_cache). The other non-tier policies of T-618..T-621 (breakeven, bayes, survival, ghost, phase, lecar) were also run uncapped: their gaps to the tier carry the same caveat | n/a | INFO (corrects the T-618..T-621 reading); the open question is the cap with the rule: spec 117 (swaps 32 / 64 / 96, with and without STRATA_ADAPT_KMISS=2,4) is queued as chain 16 | bench/results/2026-11-16-tier-laws/cap32.{txt,json} |

| T-651 | 2026-11-16 | fork bug fix (a): STRATA_CONV_DISK_WANTS=1, a STRATA_CONV_DISK_ONLY save obeys --conversation-cache-min-tokens (ElXreno 3da39ce1, LEADS.md Part H) | src/program/fork_bugfix_rules_test.cpp (ctest fork_bugfix_rules_test): disk_only_save_allowed; clang++ host build and run, 29 checks (all six rules) | Windows box, no GPU | host build (clang++) of the helper and test only; engine not built or run | CLAIM HOLDS (read in our tree): the disk-only branch of park_current_body and the QUIT save called disk_save_live with no conversations.wants(live.size()) (the RAM park checks it), so a conversation under --conversation-cache-min-tokens was written to disk. Fix: both call sites ask disk_only_save_allowed(STRATA_CONV_DISK_WANTS, wants). Unset: the old unconditional save. The test proves unset = save always, set = skip when !wants. NOT BUILT, NOT RUN: the engine was not built on this Windows box (no GPU); generate.cpp was not compiled in full (CUDA headers missing), only the helper and the test were. The Linux box runs the ctest. Correctness only: no spec (no speed effect). | exact (which conversations are written, never their bytes) | CODED default off; ctest pending on the box | no archive |
| T-652 | 2026-11-16 | fork bug fix (b): STRATA_CACHE_ROOT_PAST=1, prompt-cache root at the first turn boundary at or past --prompt-cache-root (ElXreno 00be27a0, LEADS.md Part H) | ctest fork_bugfix_rules_test: prompt_cache_root_at, old rule vs new rule on a short-system-prompt / long-first-message id list | Windows box, no GPU | host build (clang++) of the helper and test only; engine not built or run | CLAIM HOLDS: the search took the first turn token after position 0 and kept it only if it lay at or past the minimum (default 2048), so a short system prompt followed by a long first user message gave no root at all. Fix: the search starts at the minimum (unset: the old search). The fork changed a second site (the live conversation first_turn at its line 12312); our tree has no such site (one use of prompt_cache_root, generate.cpp). The test proves old gives -1 and new gives the boundary at or past the minimum, and both agree when the first boundary is already past it. NOT BUILT, NOT RUN (engine). Whether the new root helps real agent traffic is untested: it changes where a checkpoint is placed, so it needs an A/B on the cache spec before any default; correctness-only for now, no spec written. | exact (a checkpoint position, the K/V of the same tokens) | CODED default off; ctest pending on the box | no archive |

| T-793 | 2026-11-17 | T-630 root cause by reading, UNCONFIRMED STATIC ANALYSIS (nothing run on a GPU): with `STRATA_PF_HC_REGION=1` (set by the preset; the chunk line of T-792 says `STRATA_PF_HC_REGION on`) `Prefill::carve` takes xn16 / lo / lo16 / gated from the START of the region that the MoE set (logits, w, ids, slot_dev, src_dev, GU, Dm, shared, sg, row_sd) also starts at, so xn16 (T x (D+pad) x 2 bytes, 47 MB at T 2304) lies over logits / w / ids / slot_dev; the unfused path is safe (the combine kernel has ended before gr_write_norm_rs writes xn16, the invariant the `pf_hc_region` comment states) but `moe_combine_write_norm_rs` writes xn16 in block t while later blocks still read slot_dev / w / Dm, so a slot index becomes image bytes and a later block reads Dm out of bounds; the asynchronous fault surfaces at the next checked launch (`dequant launch`). Not covered by combine_write_parity (separate buffers) | reading of prefill.cpp (carve ~2371-2442, gate ~7235), kernels.cu moe_combine_write_norm_rs; fix: the gate refuses the fused kernel under `pf_hc_region()` (`include/strata/prefill/combine_write_rule.hpp`, default path byte-identical); `python tools/pancake/test_disk_env_table.py` | Windows box, no GPU | offline | clang++ (ROCm 6.2, gfx1100) -fsyntax-only of prefill.cpp and combine_write_parity.cu: 0 errors; host `combine_write_rule_test` (new): ok; `test_disk_env_table.py`: 3 tests OK; combine_write_parity gained T 2304 / 2245 / 2304 f16 cases with a 64 KiB canary after R, rs, xn16 and xn16_lo (NOT RUN, no GPU here) | n/a | CODE, box rerun NOT MEASURED (queued): ctest combine_write_parity + combine_write_rule_test, then spec 119 arms pf-combine-write (now equals base: refused under HC_REGION), pf-combine-write-no-hc-region (fused kernel, `STRATA_PF_HC_REGION=0`), pf-combine-write-serial-edit | none (no run) |

| T-843 | 2026-11-17 | strata-poly prompt: where the GPU time of a 1800-token prompt goes (nsys 2024.6 `-t cuda`) plus the static reading of src/poly/engine.cpp moe_rows (unconfirmed static analysis) | `nsys profile -t cuda python bench_win_prompt.py 1 1 4 2048 1800` (2 requests of 1800 tokens: warm-up + measured), then `nsys stats -r cuda_gpu_kern_sum,cuda_gpu_mem_time_sum` | same build, model, card as T-842 | 1 profile | total GPU kernel time 17.9 s for the 2 requests (about 75% of their 24 s wall). By ggml type of the llama.cpp MMQ kernel (mul_mat_q, int8): IQ2_XXS (gate/up experts) 6.36 s = 35.6%, IQ3_XXS (down experts) 3.04 s = 17.0%, IQ4_XS + IQ2_S (4 other layers' experts) 0.30 s: the routed experts are about 54% of the GPU kernel time; Q5_K 3.87 s = 21.6% and Q6_K 1.07 s = 6.0% are DENSE weights (attention qkv/gate/output, shared experts: gguf tensor list) that an expert-only F16 path would not touch; gdn_state_cols 0.48 s, cuBLAS f16 gemms (attention) about 1.2 s. Host-to-Device copies 9.17 s (8,352 copies: the staging of experts, `stage()` engine.cpp ~2440) against 0.06 s device-to-host. Static: the prompt MoE is moe_rows (engine.cpp:2449): every routed expert, VRAM-resident or staged, runs through llama.cpp's MMQ with int8 activations (Mmq::quantize + Mmq::mul, src/poly/mmq.cu:84); rows sorted by expert; the experts outside the 2.1 GiB VRAM cache pass through the staging buffer over PCIe, a share goes to the CPU (cpu_experts, engine.cpp ~2728); kStageRows = 32 (runtime.hpp:29) is the smallest batch on this path. A 1800-token prompt routes to nearly all 256 experts of every layer, so every batch stages most of the 78% of the experts outside the cache | n/a | INFO | bench/results/2026-11-17-gtx1660ti-f16/nsys_1800tok_x2_kernel_mem_summary.txt |

| T-868 | 2026-11-17 | owner's question: is int8 already on the matrix path of strata-poly, so that there is nothing to port? STATIC READING of the code, no run | read of CMakeLists.txt (strata-poly target from line 3762, strata-split at line 3980), src/poly/mmq.cu, src/poly/engine.cpp (`L.mmq`), src/split/src/dense.cuh hc.cuh moe_q4k.cuh kernels.cu attn_prompt_i8.cuh, grep of `__dp4a` | host-only, no run | n/a | YES for strata-poly, with a correction on where. (1) strata-poly's prompt MoE runs the routed experts through llama.cpp's MMQ kernels when a layer's rows reach kStageRows and the types fit (`L.mmq = opt_.gpu_experts && rows_ >= kStageRows && Mmq::fits(...)`, engine.cpp:1915; library `strata_poly_mmq` = src/poly/mmq.cu + the ggml-cuda mmq template instances for q4_0 .. q6_k, iq1_s, iq2_xxs, iq2_xs, iq2_s, iq3_xxs, iq3_s, iq4_nl, iq4_xs): these are int8 dot-product kernels (`__dp4a` on sm_75). (2) strata-poly also links `strata_kernels`, where src/kernels/cuda/iq_kernels.cu (138 `__dp4a` lines), native_mmvq.cu (8) and native_moe.cu (1) hold int8 matvec code. CORRECTION to the list the owner gave: src/split/src/dense.cuh, hc.cuh, moe_q4k.cuh (`__dp4a` on 8 / 9 / 4 lines) and attn_prompt_i8.cuh (FNX_PFX_ATTN_I8) belong to `strata-split`, the opt-in multi-GPU engine (OFF by default, Linux + CUDA, no other target depends on it), NOT to strata-poly. FNX_PFX_ATTN_I8 is compiled only for sm_80+ (`#if !FNX_HIP && FNX_ARCH >= 80`, kernels.cu:1449) and defaults on only for the IQ3_S-type files (`attn_i8_default()`), so it does not run on a GTX 1660 Ti in any case. Nothing int8 is left to port to strata-poly; the f16 alternative was closed in T-844 (int8 6.9 TOPS against f16 wmma 1.59 TFLOPS on TU116). All strata-poly flags were already tested with no gain (T-786, T-787) | n/a | INFO (static reading; the measured side is T-843, the nsys profile of the poly prompt, which shows the MMQ kernels) | no archive |

Everything before it passed: reserve (zero chunk mapped at all 4 chunk addresses), the D2H read of 4 chunks (zeros), the real chunk mapped over chunk 1, the hipMemset of 9 through it, and the shape check (chunk 1 = 9s, chunks 0 / 2 / 3 = zeros), and unmap() returned a handle with mapped_count 0 and a successful copy. So the plain range, the zero chunk's clearing through alias 0 (all aliases see it), read-only access plus D2H over aliases, and a first zero-to-real remap of a chunk address all WORK. What breaks: after `VmmRange::unmap(1)` (hipMemUnmap of the real chunk, then at once `zero_map(1)` = hipMemMap of the zero chunk at the same address, then `set_access_ro(1, 2)`) the read of that address returns bytes that are not all zero. HYPOTHESES, ranked (vmm.cpp HIP branch): H1 address reuse after hipMemUnmap: the address is unmapped and re-mapped to another handle with no work in between and the old translation is still used (stale page table / TLB), so the read still returns the 9s (`VmmRange::unmap`, the hipMemUnmap + `zero_map` pair; the zero-to-real remap of step 4 worked, so it would be specific to dropping a written RW mapping); H2 `zero_map(1)` failed and the error was swallowed: `unmap()` ignores its result and `zero_map` did not log (now it does, `fail("hipMemMap (zero chunk)")`), the address stays unmapped, `set_access_ro` is skipped, and the D2H read of an unmapped address returns stale data instead of an error; H3 the host-copy path (hipMemcpy D2H of a read-only mapped range through a blit / SDMA engine) reads the old physical pages while a device-side read would not; H4 the permission change RW -> RO on the same address after a remap is applied lazily or not at all (`set_access_ro`); H5 the bytes are not the 9s but uninitialised memory (a fresh mapping is not guaranteed zero) which would point at the zero chunk itself, but chunks 0 / 2 / 3 read zeros one step earlier, so this is the least likely. NOT KNOWN: the actual values read (all 9? garbage?), whether the failure is deterministic or timing-dependent (the test copies right after the remap, with no sync), whether the retry after a sync / pause reads zeros, whether the device-to-device path sees the same bytes, whether `zero_map` returned an error, whether another chunk index fails the same way, and how the plain (non-zero-backed) path behaves at the same address reuse (chain T's other vmm tests passed, expert_cache_segmented_vmmrange_test passed). CODE CHANGES, CODED, NOT YET RUN ON GPU (not a fix until the box confirms; default behaviour byte-identical, the CUDA branch and CPU untouched; HIP branch and test clean under ROCm 6.2 clang++ -fsyntax-only, test also under cl /Zs with CUDA 12.8 headers): (1) src/core/vmm.cpp HIP branch, only with STRATA_VMM_ZERO_BACK=1: `zero_setup()` logs one stderr line per decision (granularity, chunk count, zero chunk create, number of chunk addresses mapped, per stage: read-write access / memset / sync return strings, read-only access, self-check result with the first bad chunk / offset / value); stage 1 clears the shared chunk through address 0, and when the read-back of 16 bytes at start / middle / end of EVERY chunk is not zero, stage 2 clears it through EVERY address (read-write on all, memset each, sync, read-only on all) and reads back again; still wrong or any call failed: every zero mapping is undone, the chunk freed, the range is plain and `zero_fell_back()` is true, with one stderr line why. `unmap()` now syncs first, and after the zero chunk comes back it reads the chunk back; a wrong read retries once (unmap + map + read-only again), a second wrong read calls `zero_disable()` (zero backing of the whole range undone, real chunks kept, one stderr line). So a first run either has working zero backing proven by read-back or a safely disabled lever, never bytes that are not zeros. (2) tests/core/vmm_test.cpp: the zero-back section is 6 independent named steps, each printing PASS / FAIL / SKIPPED; a wrong read prints the first wrong byte, chunk, value, expected value and wrong-byte count per chunk; when step 5 fails it also re-reads after sync + 200 ms, copies device-to-device and re-reads the whole range; when the lever undid itself the run prints FALLBACK (stderr has why), skips the steps that need zeros, and the test passes (safe outcome, NOT a pass of the zero backing). (3) tools/pancake/vmm_probe.cpp probe 5b: the whole cycle on 4 addresses (zero chunk everywhere, real chunk over address 1, zero chunk back, read at once / after sync / device copy / after unmap + map again), each read printed as zeros or NOT ZERO with the first bad byte. | T-987 | 2026-11-19 | FAILED RD QUEUE (full Strata-rdna3 config) + CAUSE + FIX: run-queue-rd2.sh 21:23-21:38 box clock MEASURED NOTHING in all three steps: (1) bench-updatedRD.v7.sh: CONFIG ok, plain build ok, plain smoke 21:28 "server died before ready" (WINBENCHRD_ABORTED, the chain still exits 0); (2) bench-exact.v3.sh RD part: 252-screen exit 2, "server died before ready"; (3) bench-rdgate.v2.sh: ctest-plain exit 8 (parity binaries not built), task gate "the server died twice before ready". CAUSE (reproduced, engine log line `strata serve: mtp: writing the draft head`, exit 1, right after `strata mtp: draft head over 59607 tokens (77.3 MiB)`): Strata-rdna3's `--mtp-q4 all` includes the draft-head copy (src/core/mtp.cpp make_q4_head), which requantizes the draft head's rows to Q4_0 and cudaMemcpy's them back IN PLACE into the buffer sized for the head's own rows (comment: "they are smaller"). The box's IQ2_XS GGUF has output.weight IQ4_XS [2560 x 248320] = 1360 bytes per row; Q4_0 needs 1440, so the write overruns (59607 x 1440 B into 59607 x 1360 B) and fails. Their own model IQ3_S has output.weight Q6_K (2100 B per row), where it fits. The MTP pack is not the cause: their setup uses the same <data>/mtp/rt (rdna3/run.py: data/mtp/rt/experts.bin), the one on the box; there is no separate q4 MTP file (--mtp-q4 makes its Q4_0 copies at load). Bisect (one engine start each, abtest smoke as the chain runs it, /home/dan/auto-runs/rdfix-bisect/): t02 full config FAIL (same line); t03 only `--mtp-q4 proj` PASS, decode 81.9 tok/s on one short request (smoke, not a measurement); t04 only `--mtp-q4 head` FAIL (same line). FIX (owner rule: their flags, never overridden except a model-file dependency): bench_updatedRD_conf.py reads output.weight's type from the substituted model's GGUF header and, only when the engine's Q4_0 row is larger than the head's row, substitutes `--mtp-q4 all -> proj` (head -> removed), logged as a CONFIG SUBST line with this reason; every other argument and env key of theirs stays; an unreadable GGUF keeps their value (UNCHECKED line); the updated arm is built on the same plain base. Spec 250 note says so. PROTOCOL CLARIFIED BY THE OWNER ~10:55-11:05 (supersedes the 3-rep confirm stage and the preliminary comparison; run-queue-us5.sh, scripts bench-sweep.v5.sh / bench-retest.v4.sh, new harness pin = the commit of this row): (1) ALL flags get ONE run (the screen, slice 1 = usS1-1of5 running since 10:31); (2) a RESCREEN = a SECOND independent 1-run test of the flags that showed a benefit (any case > 0 %, ranked by gain, HIGH >= 2.0 % right after their slice, LOW 0-2 % after the final comparison), blocked like the screen (blocks of 8, base before and base-late after, the slice's first start is the only warm-up, other seed = other block neighbours and order, last-request drift > 5 % => one re-run, then INCONCLUSIVE and one more rescreen); (3) NO separate 3-rep confirm stage: the verdict comes from run 1 + run 2 together (sweep.py two_run): STANDS = KEEP when the benefit repeats in the same direction (both runs > 0 on the case(s) that carried it; effect = mean of the two runs, spread = their difference), OPTIONAL when a case >= +10 % and a case <= -3 % both repeat in both runs, NO-GAIN = DROP when run 2 does not repeat the benefit or both are <= 0, MIXED (nothing ships) when the gain repeats but a case lost >= 3 % in both runs, INCONCLUSIVE only on drift; apply_triage reads the two-run record (pass2 = mean, pass2_clean) exactly like a 3-rep record; a flag with no benefit in run 1 stays NO-GAIN-P1 (no shipped change, one run is no refutation); (4) NO stock arm in the flag work (the base is the updated arm), the PRELIMINARY stock comparison is REMOVED (stock vs updated exists since 2026-11-19, T-991..T-995); stock appears only in the FINAL 5-rep comparison (exact + lossy, with the gate), which validates the whole shipped set; quality gates only for --lookup-max-t and lossy flags with a two-run mean >= 3 % (verdict RETEST-GATE). ORDER: screen 1 -> rescreen of its HIGH flags -> screen 2 -> rescreen -> ... -> screen 5 -> rescreen -> gates -> FINAL comparison -> LOW-gain flags (rescreen only, same rule). ETAs (assumption ~6 HIGH flags per slice = ONE rescreen block of 9 starts, 0.5 h per slice): slice 1 ends ~12:45, its rescreen ~13:15, screens + rescreens end ~22:30, gates ~00:00, FINAL ~03:15 next day (the 3-rep blocks and the preliminary comparison, ~6 h, are gone), the LOW-gain rescreens (about 90 flags in 4 slices) ~11:00. TRIAGE CORRECTED BY THE OWNER ~11:10 (run-queue-us6.sh supersedes us5; sweep.py classifies run 1 by the PER-CASE effects short / edit / long64k prompt / long64k decode): HIGH = ANY case >= +2 % (even when another case is negative: a big gain on one case can end OPTIONAL) -> RESCREEN; LOW = best case > 0 % and < 2 % -> NOT rescreened this round: BACKLOG (us-backlog-low.json with the run-1 numbers, next week with the 31 deferred switches; the low-gain rescreen stage is removed); NEGATIVE = every case <= 0 %: a plus arm (off-by-default flag) is NO-GAIN, dropped; a minus arm (preset flag, having it ON hurts) is HURTS and is rescreened once (no shipped removal from one run: DROP only when run 2 agrees, else UNSTABLE = nothing changes). The rescreen (run 2) is only for HIGH + HURTS flags; the two-run rule decides KEEP / OPTIONAL / DROP. Order: screen 1 (running) -> rescreen(HIGH + HURTS) -> screen 2 -> rescreen -> ... screen 5 -> rescreen -> gates (--lookup-max-t, lossy >= 3 %) -> FINAL comparison.
